Oh, um... well, I wouldn't say I'm completely exhausted or anything. I don't want to make it sound worse than it is, because I know people get tired as they get older, right? I'm 64, so maybe it's just my age.

Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to be a burden on anyone, and I know everyone makes mistakes, but... 

I just keep thinking about work. I manage these properties, you know, and lately I just feel so awful about things I've said to the residents. Sometimes I speak a little too big, I guess? And then I just sit there and feel terrible, like I've completely let them down and failed them. Even though, honestly, nobody has actually come out and said I ruined anything or that they're mad at me. But I just feel this heavy guilt about it anyway, like I'm doing a terrible job.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... well... I suppose I do feel a little guilty sometimes. I really hope you don't think less of me for saying this, but... lately, I just keep thinking about the association, and how I might have let the residents down. 

I tend to, well, speak a bit too big sometimes, you know? I promised them some new things for the neighborhood before I really sat down and checked the money properly. And now I just have this awful feeling hanging over me that I've messed it all up. 

No one has actually said I ruined anything, they've all been very sweet, but I just feel so terrible about it. It makes me feel like I'm not doing a good job for them, and I pride myself on doing a good job. I'm so sorry, I know it's probably silly to worry so much about it. Does that make sense, Doctor? Am I explaining it right?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... yes, actually. I hope it doesn't make me sound like I'm losing my touch, because I really do try my best to keep everything organized for the association. 

But lately, my head just feels a bit... cloudy, I suppose you could say. I'll be staring right at a board packet, or trying to read an email from one of our vendors, and... I just can't seem to make up my mind. Even on the simple little things that I used to handle so quickly, you know? It's like I just can't get my thoughts to line up the way they're supposed to. 

I'm so sorry, I know I should be sharper than that, especially with people relying on me. I really hope I'm not doing a terrible job. Does that make sense, Doctor? Is that the kind of thing you meant?

I hope it's okay that I'm telling you this... is that normal to feel that way?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you no longer take pleasure in those activities, or do you still enjoy them but just don’t feel like doing them right now?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I'm so sorry, Doctor, I'm not entirely sure how to answer that. I hope I'm not being difficult by not knowing. \n\nUm... let me think. I guess... maybe I just don't feel like doing them right now? Like, I'm sure if I actually forced myself to go down to the galleries, I'd probably still think the art was real pretty, and I know I love my family just as much as ever. I just... I just can't seem to find the get-up-and-go to actually do the things. \n\nI feel so silly not knowing for sure how to explain my own feelings to you. I'm probably just being lazy, honestly. I guess I have been feeling a little more drained than usual. Even if I sleep okay, I wake up and just feel sort of... heavy? Like my batteries aren't quite charged all the way. I just have to push myself a lot harder to get through the day. \n\nI'm probably just not exercising enough or something. Do you think that's it, Doctor? I hope I'm not complaining too much about normal things.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty or like you’re letting people down more than usual, even when you know logically that you’re not?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to be a burden on anyone, and I know everyone makes mistakes, but... \n\nI just keep thinking about work. I manage these properties, you know, and lately I just feel so awful about things I've said to the residents. Sometimes I speak a little too big, I guess? Like, I'll promise them we can fix something or upgrade something before I actually check the budget to see if the money is there. \n\nAnd then I just sit there and feel terrible, like I've completely let them down and failed them. Even though, honestly, nobody has actually come out and said I ruined anything or that they're mad at me. But I just feel this heavy guilt about it anyway, like I'm doing a terrible job. \n\nI'm probably just being too hard on myself, right? I hope it's okay to admit that. I just have to push myself a lot harder to get through the day. \n\nI'm probably just not exercising enough or something. Do you think that's it, Doctor? I hope I'm not complaining too much about normal things.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty or like you’re letting people down more than usual, even when you know logically that you’re not?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to be a burden on anyone, and I know everyone makes mistakes, but... \n\nI just keep thinking about work. I manage these properties, you know, and lately I just feel so awful about things I've said to the residents. Sometimes I speak a little too big, I guess? Like, I'll promise them we can fix something or upgrade something before I actually check the budget to see if the money is there. \n\nAnd then I just sit there and feel terrible, like I've completely let them down and failed them. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of examples. Does that make sense, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you just don’t feel any interest or pleasure in things that usually make you happy, even when you're not busy or tired?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess... yes, I suppose there have been some days like that. I hope it doesn't make me sound like a terrible, ungrateful person, because I know I have a lot to be thankful for. \n\nBut... I just haven't wanted to go to my dance class at all lately. And usually I just love going down and looking at the little art galleries, but I haven't even wanted to do that. I haven't even been cooking Sunday food for my family, which... oh, I feel so bad about that. Those are all things that usually make me feel like myself, you know? But lately, even on a quiet Sunday when I'm not working, I just... don't care to do them. \n\nI'm probably just in a little funk, right? Everyone gets in a funk sometimes. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about, Doctor? I hope I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: On those days when you’re not busy or tired, do you still find yourself feeling completely uninterested in things you usually enjoy, like looking at art or cooking for your family?\n\nPatient: Um, yes... I really want to be helpful. \n\nBut yes, even when I'm just sitting at home with nothing to do, I still don't have the urge to go down to the galleries, or... or even make the Sunday dinner. And my dance class... I just haven't been going. I hope I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, even when there’s no specific reason for it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say I feel down most of the day. I mean, I have my moments, of course, like anyone does. Sometimes I might feel a little blue for a bit, but it's not all day, every day. \n\nI try really hard to keep a positive attitude, you know? I don't want to be one of those people who just mopes around all the time. I'm usually pretty good at just pulling myself up by my bootstraps and getting on with things. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say it's a constant thing. Is that... is that okay? I just don't get that same good, satisfying feeling from them right now. \n\nI feel so guilty even saying it out loud again, it makes me sound so lazy and ungrateful, doesn't it? I promise I'm usually a very active, involved person. I'm sure it's just a temporary little slump and I'll be back to my old self in no time. Does that... does that answer your question a little better?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling sad, down, or depressed most of the day, nearly every day — even if it's just a quiet sort of sadness that's always in the background?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I'm so sorry, Doctor. I feel terrible making you ask me this again. I must not be very good at explaining things, or maybe I'm just getting confused. \n\nBut... no, I really don't have a sadness like that hanging over me. I know I mentioned losing interest in the cooking and the galleries, but... as for just feeling sad all day, even quietly... no, I don't think so. I really do try to be a cheerful person. I have my tenants and the community association to look after, so I just keep busy and keep a smile on, you know? I don't like to let things get me down. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't have that to report. I don't want you to think I'm hiding anything from you, I promise I'm not. I just want to be as honest and helpful as I can. Am I doing alright? I hope I'm giving you what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood is worse in the morning, even after you've just woken up?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no... I don't think so. I mean, mornings are actually when I try to be my most productive, you know? I like to get up early and get a head start on the day, maybe do a little tidying or check in on some of the association emails. \n\nI wouldn't say I wake up feeling worse at all. If anything, I try to wake up with a good attitude, ready to tackle whatever needs doing. I don't want to start the day off on the wrong foot, that just sets a bad tone for everyone, doesn't it? \n\nSo, no, mornings are usually quite fine for me. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly. Am I being helpful, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling worthless or guilty almost every day, even about small things?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well... I suppose I do feel a little guilty sometimes. I really hope you don't think less of me for saying this, but... lately, I just keep thinking about the association, and how I might have let the residents down. \n\nI tend to, well, speak a bit too big sometimes, you know? I promised them some new things for the neighborhood before I really sat down and checked the money properly. And now I just have this awful feeling hanging over me that I've messed it all up. \n\nNo one has actually said I ruined anything, they've all been very sweet, but I just feel so terrible about it. It makes me feel like I'm not doing a good job for them, and I pride myself on doing a good job. I'm so sorry, I know it's probably silly to worry so much about it. Does that make sense, Doctor? Am I explaining it right?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, like your dance class or cooking for your family, even when you have the time and energy to do them?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... yes, I think I might have mentioned the cooking and the galleries a little bit earlier. I hope it's okay that I bring them up again, I don't want to sound like a broken record. \n\nBut to be perfectly honest—and I feel a bit bad admitting this, because I really should be keeping active—I just haven't wanted to go to my dance class at all lately. Or even go look at the little galleries like I usually do. And the cooking... well, I usually love making the big Sunday food for my family. It's just... those are the things that usually make me feel like myself, you know? And lately, even when I have a free afternoon, I just haven't had the heart for any of it. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound lazy, or like I don't care about my family, because I love them dearly. I just haven't felt up to it. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to focus or concentrate on tasks, like reading or making decisions, more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... yes, actually. I hope it doesn't make me sound like I'm losing my touch, because I really do try my best to keep everything organized for the association. \n\nBut lately, my head just feels a bit... cloudy, I suppose you could say. I'll be staring right at a board packet, or trying to read an email from one of our vendors, and... I just can't seem to make up my mind. Even on the simple little things that I used to handle so quickly, you know? It's like I just can't get my thoughts to line up the way they're supposed to. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I should be sharper than that, especially with people relying on me. I really hope I'm not doing a terrible job. Does that make sense, Doctor? Is that the kind of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you no longer feel any pleasure or happiness from activities you used to enjoy, like your dance class or cooking for your family, even when you do them?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, I know I touched on this just a moment ago, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much. I just want to be as helpful as I can. \n\nBut yes, even if I do try to force myself to go to the dance class or... or look at the galleries, it just doesn't feel the same. The joy just isn't there right now. And the Sunday dinners... I made a roast last week because I didn't want the family to worry, but I didn't feel that usual warmth in my heart while I was doing it. \n\nThose are the things that usually make me feel like myself, and I feel just awful that I'm not enjoying them the way I should be. I really hope that doesn't make me sound ungrateful for the lovely life I have. I know I should be happier. Am I giving you the right kind of information, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that even when something good happens, like spending time with family or getting a compliment, it doesn’t really lift your mood or make you feel happy?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I wouldn't say that. I hope I haven't given you the impression that I'm completely miserable all the time, because I really do appreciate the good things in my life. \n\nWhen my grandchildren come over, or if one of the board members tells me I've done a nice job on the newsletter... well, it does bring a little smile to my face. It might not be quite as bright as it used to be, but... I do still feel a little spark of gladness when nice things happen. I really try to focus on those moments, you know? \n\nI'm not just sitting around ignoring all the blessings I have. I really hope that makes sense, Doctor. Is that a good sign, that I can still feel a little bit of happiness when good things happen?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that even when you do things you used to enjoy, like seeing your grandchildren or going to your dance class, you feel completely disconnected or numb, like you’re just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I wouldn't say I feel completely numb. I hope I haven't made it sound like I'm entirely shut off from the world, because I really do try my best to be present, especially for my family. \n\nWhen I see my grandchildren, or even when I just go to the dance class... I'm not just a robot going through the motions. I might not feel that big burst of joy I used to, like I mentioned before, but I'm still there, and I still care very much. I don't feel disconnected from them. I just... I suppose it's just that the feeling is a bit muted right now. \n\nBut I'm certainly not numb to the people I love. I really hope that's alright to say.
